n\in\mathbb{N}
, so that
\lambda^{(n)}{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
. By Proposition
4.1
, the sequence
(u(\lambda^{(n)}))
is bounded in
\mathcal{M}
by a constant
c
. Define the ball

\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c):=\{x\in\mathcal{M}:\|x\|_{\mathcal{M}}\leq c\}.

Thus

\{u(\lambda^{(n)}):n\in\mathbb{N}\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c).

By
[
21
, Theorems 3.6.3 and 3.6.11]
, any closed, bounded ball in a Hilbert space is not only compact for the weak topology on
\mathcal{M}
, but also metrizable for the weak topology on
\mathcal{M}
. By
[
21
, Theorem 2.3.6]
, since
\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
is metrizable and weakly compact,
\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
is weakly sequentially compact. Recall, for a metric space
\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
,
\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
is sequentially compact if every sequence has a convergent subsequence. Since
\{u((1-1/n)\tau)\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
, there is a subsequence
(z_{n})
of
(1-1/n)
such that
z_{n}\tau\rightarrow\tau
and
u(z_{n}\tau)
converges to some element
x\in\mathrm{Ball}(\mathcal{M},c)
. Therefore there exists a sequence
z_{n}\rightarrow 1
such that
u(z_{n}\tau)
tends to
x
weakly in
\mathcal{M}
. By Theorem
2.12
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})u(\lambda)=\gamma.

We conclude that
(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})x=\gamma
. Since
D\tau_{P}
is a contraction,
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})\perp\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. Hence
\gamma\perp\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
, and it follows from equation (
2.4
) that, for all
z\in\mathbb{D}
,

u(z\tau)=(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})(1_{\mathcal{M}}-zD\tau_{P})^{-1}x,

and so
u(z\tau)\perp\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. Hence
x\perp\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. We have shown that there is a vector
x\in\mathcal{M}
with the properties that
(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})x=\gamma
and
x\perp\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. Since such a vector is unique, we deduce the first assertion of the theorem by taking
u(\tau)=x
.

To see equation (
4.14
), suppose that
z_{n}\rightarrow 1
and
u(z_{n}\tau)\rightarrow v
. Then
v\perp\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
and
(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})v=\gamma
. Hence
v=u(\tau)
.

For any
r\in(0,1)
, we have by the model formula

\dfrac{1-\lvert\varphi(r\tau)\rvert^{2}}{1-r^{2}}=\|u(r\tau)\|_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}.

As
r\rightarrow 1^{-}
, the right hand side of the above equation tends to
\|u(\tau)\|^{2}
, and, by equation (
4.14
), the left hand side tends to the lim inf in equation (
4.15
), by Corollary
3.5
and Proposition
4.4
. This establishes equation (
4.15
).∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},u)
be a model of
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
and let
(a,\beta,\gamma,D)
be a realization of
(\mathcal{M},u)
. A point
\tau\in\partial\mathbb{D}^{d}
is a
B
-point for
\varphi
if and only if
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
.

Proof.

If
\tau
is a
B
-point then Theorem
4.5
states that there exists a
u_{\tau}\in\mathcal{M}
such that
(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})u(\tau)=\gamma
, and hence
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. Conversely, suppose that
(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})x=\gamma
for some
x\in\mathcal{M}
. Then, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

\displaystyle u(\lambda)

\displaystyle=(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}\gamma=(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})x

\displaystyle=(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P}+D(\lambda_{P}-\tau_{P}))x

\displaystyle=x+(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}D(\lambda_{P}-\tau_{P})x.

Since
D
is a contraction,

\|(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}D\|=\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(D\lambda_{P})^{j}D\bigg\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\lambda\|_{\infty}^{j}=\dfrac{1}{1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}},

and so

\displaystyle\|u(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|_{\mathcal{M}}+\|(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\lambda_{P})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}\cdot\|(\lambda_{P}-\tau_{P})x\|_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|_{\mathcal{M}}+\dfrac{\|\lambda-\tau\|_{\infty}}{1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}}\|x\|_{\mathcal{M}}.

Hence, for all
\lambda\in S{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
,

\|u(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{M}}\leq(1+c)\|x\|_{\mathcal{M}}.

Thus
u(\lambda)
is bounded on
S
, and by Corollary
4.3
,
\tau
is a
B
-point of
\varphi
.∎

5.
Interactions of carapoints with models of Schur-Agler class functions

Our next task is to show that if a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
has a singularity at a
B
-point
\tau
, then we can construct a generalized model of
\varphi
in which the singularity of
\varphi
is encoded in an
I(\lambda)
that is singular at
\tau
, in such a way that the model has a
C
-point at
\tau
. The device that leads to this conclusion is to write vectors in and operators on
\mathcal{M}
in terms of the orthogonal decomposition
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
where
\mathcal{N}=\ker(1-D\tau_{P})
and
D
comes from a realization of
(\mathcal{M},u)
. The following observation is straightforward.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a separable Hilbert space with orthogonal decomposition

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{M}_{2}\ldots\oplus\mathcal{M}_{d}.

For
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, let us define the operator

\tau_{P}=\tau_{1}P_{1}+\ldots+\tau_{d}P_{d},

where
P_{j}:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
is the orthogonal projection from
\mathcal{M}
onto
\mathcal{M}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. It is easy to see that
\tau_{P}
is a unitary operator,

\displaystyle\tau_{P}^{*}\tau_{P}

\displaystyle=\overline{\tau}_{P}\tau_{P}=\lvert\tau_{1}\rvert^{2}P_{1}+\ldots+\lvert\tau_{d}\rvert^{2}P_{d}=P_{1}+\ldots+P_{d}=1_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\tau_{P}\tau^{*}_{P}

\displaystyle=\tau_{P}\overline{\tau}_{P}=\lvert\tau_{1}\rvert^{2}P_{1}+\ldots+\lvert\tau_{d}\rvert^{2}P_{d}=P_{1}+\ldots+P_{d}=1_{\mathcal{M}}.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a complex Hilbert space and let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
. Then if
X=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of operators on
\mathcal{M}
, we define the operator

\lambda_{X}=\lambda_{1}X_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}X_{d}.

Realizations provide an effective tool for the study of boundary behavior. Here is a preliminary observation.

Lemma 5.2
.

[
5
, Lemma 3.3]

Suppose that
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
has a model
(\mathcal{M},u)
with realization
(a,\beta,\gamma,D)
. Let
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
be a carapoint of
\varphi
, let
\mathcal{N}=\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
,
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
. Then

\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})\subset\mathcal{N}^{\perp}~~\text{and}~~\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\in\mathcal{N}^{\perp}.

Proof.

First we show that
\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\in\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
. Let

L=\begin{bmatrix}a&1\otimes\beta\\
\gamma\otimes 1&D\end{bmatrix}.

Choose any
x\in\mathcal{N}
. Then
x=D\tau_{P}x
and so

L\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\tau_{P}x\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}a&1\otimes\beta\\
\gamma\otimes 1&D\\
\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\tau_{P}x\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\langle\tau_{P}x,\beta\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}\\
D\tau_{P}x\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\langle x,\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}\\
x\end{bmatrix}.

Since
L
is a contraction and
\tau_{P}
is an isometry,

\Bigg\|\begin{bmatrix}\langle x,\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}\\
x\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\|=\Bigg\|L\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\tau_{P}x\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\|\leq\Bigg\|\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\tau_{P}x\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\|=\|\tau_{P}x\|_{\mathcal{N}}=\|x\|_{\mathcal{N}},

and so
\langle x,\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}=0
. Since
x\in\mathcal{N}
is arbitrary,
\overline{\tau_{P}}\beta\in\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
.

Proposition
4.6
asserts that
\tau
is a carapoint for
\varphi
if and only if
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})
. Since
D\tau_{P}
is a contraction on
\mathcal{M}
, then by

[
15
, Proposition 3.1]
, for an eigenvector
x
of
D\tau_{P}
with eigenvalue
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}
, then
x
is also an eigenvector of
(D\tau_{P})^{*}
with eigenvalue
\overline{\lambda}\in\mathbb{T}
. Hence
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})=\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\overline{\tau_{P}}D^{*})
. Furthermore, by
[
19
, Lemma 6.11]
,

\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\overline{\tau_{P}}D^{*})=\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})^{\perp}.

Hence

\Big(\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})^{\perp}\Big)^{\perp}=\overline{\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})}=\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\overline{\tau_{P}}D^{*})^{\perp}.

Therefore

\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})\subset\overline{\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})}=\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\overline{\tau_{P}}D^{*})^{\perp}=\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{M}}-D\tau_{P})^{\perp}=\mathcal{N}^{\perp}.

∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{M}_{d}
be a complex Hilbert space,
P_{j}:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
be the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
and let
\mathcal{N}
be a closed subspace of
\mathcal{M}
. Then with respect to the decomposition
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
, the operator
P_{j}
has the operator matrix form

P_{j}=\begin{bmatrix}X_{j}&B_{j}\\
B^{*}_{j}&Y_{j}\end{bmatrix},

(5.1)

for some
X_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N})
,
Y_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp})
and
B_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},\mathcal{N})
for all integers such that
1\leq j\leq d
. Moreover, for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

(1)

0\leq X_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{N}}
,
0\leq Y_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
;

(2)

\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}},~\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0,~\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
;

(3)

B_{i}Y_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}-X_{i}B_{j}
,
B_{i}^{*}X_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{i}B_{j}^{*}
;

(4)

B_{i}B^{*}_{j}=\delta_{ij}X_{j}-X_{i}X_{j},~B^{*}_{i}B_{j}=\delta_{ij}Y_{j}-Y_{i}Y_{j}
,

where
\delta_{ij}
denotes the Kronecker delta.

Proof.

(1) Let
P_{j}:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
be the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. We may represent the operator
P_{j}
on
\mathcal{M}
by an operator matrix with respect to the orthogonal decomposition
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
as follows. Let

P_{j}=\begin{bmatrix}X_{j}&B_{j}\\
C_{j}&Y_{j}\end{bmatrix},

for some operators
X_{j},B_{j},C_{j},Y_{j}
. Since
P_{j}
is an orthogonal projection,
P_{j}=P_{j}^{*}
, and so
X_{j}=X^{*}_{j},B^{*}_{j}=C_{j}
and
Y_{j}=Y^{*}_{j}
. Define
P_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
to be the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{N}
. By equation (
5.1
), for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

X_{j}=P_{\mathcal{N}}P_{j}P_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}.

Note
0\leq P_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{M}}
. Hence

0\leq P_{\mathcal{N}}P_{j}P_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}=X_{j}\leq P_{\mathcal{N}}P_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}=1_{\mathcal{N}},

and similarly for
Y_{j}
.

For all
x\in\mathcal{M}
,
\|P_{j}x\|\leq\|x\|
. Choose a
u\in\mathcal{N}
, then

\|P_{j}u\|^{2}=\Bigg\|\begin{bmatrix}X_{j}u\\
B^{*}_{j}u\\
\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\|^{2}=\big\|X_{j}u\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{N}}+\big\|B^{*}_{j}u\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}\leq\|u\|^{2}_{\mathcal{N}},

and therefore
\|X_{j}\|\leq 1
for
1\leq j\leq d
. We likewise produce a similar result for
Y_{j}
with a choice of
v\in\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
.

(2) Since
P_{j}
is the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have, for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
,

P_{1}x+\ldots+P_{d}x=x.

Therefore

\sum_{j=1}^{d}P_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}},

and thus

1_{\mathcal{M}}=\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}&0\\
0&1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}\end{bmatrix}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\begin{bmatrix}X_{j}&B_{j}\\
B^{*}_{j}&Y_{j}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}&\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{d}B^{*}_{j}&\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}\end{bmatrix}.

Hence

\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}},~~\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0~~\text{and}~~\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}.

(3) Again by properties of projections,
P^{2}_{j}=P_{j}
, and so

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} X^{2}_{j}+B_{j}B^{*}_{j}&=X_{j}\\
X_{j}B_{j}+B_{j}Y_{j}&=B_{j}\\
B^{*}_{j}X_{j}+Y_{j}B^{*}_{j}&=B^{*}_{j}\\
B^{*}_{j}B_{j}+Y_{j}^{2}&=Y_{j},\end{aligned}\right.

that is,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} B_{j}B^{*}_{j}&=X_{j}-X_{j}^{2}\\
B_{j}Y_{j}&=B_{j}-X_{j}B_{j}\\
Y_{j}B^{*}_{j}&=B^{*}_{j}-B^{*}_{j}X_{j}\\
B^{*}_{j}B_{j}&=Y_{j}-Y^{2}_{j}.\end{aligned}\right.

Whereas, for
i\neq j
,
P_{i}P_{j}=0
results in

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} X_{i}X_{j}+B_{i}B^{*}_{j}&=0\\
X_{i}B_{j}+B_{i}Y_{j}&=0\\
B^{*}_{i}X_{j}+Y_{i}B^{*}_{j}&=0\\
B^{*}_{i}B_{j}+Y_{i}Y_{j}&=0,\end{aligned}\right.

that is,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} B_{i}B^{*}_{j}&=-X_{i}X_{j}\\
B_{i}Y_{j}&=-X_{i}B_{j}\\
Y_{i}B^{*}_{j}&=-B^{*}_{i}X_{j}\\
B^{*}_{i}B_{j}&=-Y_{i}Y_{j}.\end{aligned}\right.

Combing the two systems of equations (
5
) and (
5
), we see that

\displaystyle B_{i}B^{*}_{j}=\delta_{ij}X_{j}-X_{i}X_{j}

(5.4)

\displaystyle B_{i}Y_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}-X_{i}B_{j}

(5.5)

\displaystyle B_{i}^{*}X_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{i}B_{j}^{*}

(5.6)

\displaystyle B^{*}_{i}B_{j}=\delta_{ij}Y_{j}-Y_{i}Y_{j},

(5.7)

where
\delta_{ij}
denotes the Kronecker delta. ∎

These next results contain the essential tools needed to prove one of our main results, Theorem
8.2
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{M}_{d}
be a complex Hilbert space and let
\mathcal{N}
be a closed subspace of
\mathcal{M}
. Let
X=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}),B=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{d})
and
Y=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d})
be
d
-tuples of operators as in Lemma
5.3
, where
X_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N})
,
Y_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp})
and
B_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},\mathcal{N})
for all integers such that
1\leq j\leq d
, and the following conditions are satisfied

(1)

0\leq X_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{N}}
,
0\leq Y_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
;

(2)

\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}},~\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0,~\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
;

(3)

B_{i}Y_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}-X_{i}B_{j}
,
B_{i}^{*}X_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{i}B_{j}^{*}
;

(4)

B_{i}B^{*}_{j}=\delta_{ij}X_{j}-X_{i}X_{j},~B^{*}_{i}B_{j}=\delta_{ij}Y_{j}-Y_{i}Y_{j}
.

Then for all
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{B}\lambda_{B^{*}}

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{X}-\mu_{X}\lambda_{X},

\displaystyle\mu_{B^{*}}\lambda_{X}

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{B^{*}}-\mu_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}},

\displaystyle\mu_{B}\lambda_{Y}

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{B}-\mu_{X}\lambda_{B},

\displaystyle\mu_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{Y}-\mu_{Y}\lambda_{Y}.

Proof.

For all
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

\mu_{B}\lambda_{B^{*}}=\mu_{B}(\lambda_{1}B^{*}_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}B^{*}_{d})=\lambda_{1}\mu_{B}B^{*}_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}\mu_{B}B_{d}^{*}.

Note that, by equation (
5.4
),

\mu_{B}B^{*}_{j}=(\mu_{1}B_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{d}B_{d})B_{j}^{*}=\mu_{1}(\delta_{1j}X_{j}-X_{1}X_{j})+\ldots+\mu_{d}(\delta_{dj}X_{j}-X_{d}X_{j}).

(5.8)

Therefore

\displaystyle\mu_{B}\lambda_{B^{*}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{B}(\lambda_{1}B_{1}^{*}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}B^{*}_{d})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\big(\mu_{1}(X_{1}-X^{2}_{1})-\ldots-\mu_{d}X_{d}X_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt+\ldots+\lambda_{d}\big(-\mu_{1}X_{1}X_{d}+\ldots+\mu_{d}(X_{d}-X^{2}_{d})\big)

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}\lambda_{1}X_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{d}\lambda_{d}X_{d}-(\mu_{1}\lambda_{1}X^{2}_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{d}\lambda_{1}X_{d}X_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\ldots-(\mu_{1}\lambda_{d}X_{1}X_{d}+\ldots+\mu_{d}\lambda_{d}X_{d}^{2})

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{X}-\big(\mu_{X}\lambda_{1}X_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{X}\lambda_{d}X_{d}\big)

\displaystyle=(\mu\lambda)_{X}-\mu_{X}\lambda_{X}.

Similar calculations yield all the other identities.∎

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
T=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
be a d-tuple of operators on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
and
0\leq T_{j}\leq 1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. For all
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the operators

(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}~\text{and}~\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}

on
\mathcal{B(H)}
are invertible and

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{1-\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\max}\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})},

(5.9)

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}^{-1}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq~\max_{j=1,\ldots,d}\dfrac{\lvert 1-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}{1-\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})}.

(5.10)

Proof.

Let
u\in\mathcal{H}
be such that
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\lvert\langle x,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\rvert\leq\|x\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}~~\text{for all}~x\in\mathcal{H}.

By assumption,
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
and so
\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. For
u\in\mathcal{H}
with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\big\lvert\langle(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\big\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Re}\Big(\langle(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}\Bigg(\Big\langle\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathrm{Re}\Bigg((1-\lambda_{j})\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg).

(5.11)

By assumption,
0\leq T_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{H}}
, that is,
0\leq\langle T_{j}u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}
, and so

\mathrm{Re}\Big((1-\lambda_{j})\big\langle T_{j}u,u\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Big)=\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})\big\langle T_{j}u,u\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Therefore, for
u\in\mathcal{H}
with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1
, equation (
5.11
) implies

\displaystyle\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle T_{j}u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Again,
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
, so that, for
u\in\mathcal{H}
with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1

\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle T_{j}u,u\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\bigg\langle\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}u,u\bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=1.

(5.13)

Now inequality (
5.12
) and equation (
5.13
) imply

\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}u\|\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j}),

(5.14)

For all
v\in\mathcal{H}
, let
u=v/\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}}
. By equation (
5.14
),

\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(5.15)

Hence, by the open mapping theorem
[
19
, Theorem 4.43]
,
(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}
has a bounded left inverse. Furthermore, since
T_{j}=T_{j}^{*}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have
(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{*}=(\mathds{1}-\overline{\lambda})_{T}
. Therefore a similar calculation to the above shows there is a bounded left inverse for
(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{*}
. Hence
(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}
is invertible. To prove inequality (
5.9
), note that, by equation (
5.15
), for all
v\in\mathcal{H}
, there exists a unique
w\in\mathcal{H}
such that
v=(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{-1}w
and

\displaystyle\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{-1}w\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{-1}w\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(5.16)

Since by assumption,
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have
\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. Rearranging equation (
5.16
), we find that

\|(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{T}^{-1}w\|\leq\dfrac{\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}}{\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})}=\dfrac{\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}}{1-\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\max}\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})}~~\text{for all}~~w\in\mathcal{H}.

Thus inequality (
5.9
) is proved.

For the operator
\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}
, we follow a similar argument to the one above. Let
u\in\mathcal{H}
be such that
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg\lvert\bigg\langle\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u,u\bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Re}\Bigg(\bigg\langle\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u,u\bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}\Bigg(\bigg\langle\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)T_{j}u,u\bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathrm{Re}\Bigg(\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Bigg).

By assumption,
0\leq T_{j}\leq 1_{\mathcal{H}}
, that is,
0\leq\langle T_{j}u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}
. Consequently,

\mathrm{Re}\Bigg(\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\Bigg)=\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Big\langle T_{j}u,u\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Again,
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
, and so

\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle T_{j}u,u\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\bigg\langle\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}u,u\bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle u,u\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=1.

Now

\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}u\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg),

(5.17)

where by our assumption,
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
and so
\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)>0
. For all
v\in\mathcal{H}
, let
u=v/\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}}
. By equation (
5.17
),

\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}v\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(5.18)

By the open mapping theorem
[
19
, Theorem 4.43]
,

\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}
has a bounded left inverse. Furthermore, since
T_{j}=T_{j}^{*}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have
\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}^{*}=\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\lambda}}\big)_{T}
. Therefore a similar calculation to the above shows there is a bounded left inverse for
\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}^{*}
. Hence
\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}
is invertible. To prove inequality (
5.10
), note that, by inequality (
5.18
), for all
v\in\mathcal{H}
, there exists a unique
w\in\mathcal{H}
such that
v=\big(\frac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\big)_{T}^{-1}w
and

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}v\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}^{-1}w\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\geq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}^{-1}w\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(5.19)

By assumption, for
j=1,\ldots,d
,
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
and so
\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)>0
. For
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)=\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1-\overline{\lambda_{j}}}{\lvert 1-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}\bigg)=\dfrac{\mathrm{Re}(1-\overline{\lambda_{j}})}{\lvert 1-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}=\dfrac{\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})}{\lvert 1-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}.

(5.20)

Thus, by the relations (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}^{-1}w\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}}{\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\max}\dfrac{\lvert 1-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}{1-\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})}\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

This implies inequality (
5.10
). ∎

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
T=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
be a d-tuple of operators on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
and
0\leq T_{j}\leq 1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. For all
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the operators

(\lambda)_{T}~\text{and}~\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}

on
\mathcal{B(H)}
are invertible and

\displaystyle\Big\|(\lambda)_{T}^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{\underset{j=1,\ldots,d}{\min}\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})}

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}^{-1}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq~\max_{j=1,\ldots,d}\dfrac{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}{\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})}.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.5
, for
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\mu_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the operators

(\mathds{1}-\mu)_{T}~\text{and}~\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\mu}\bigg)_{T}

on
\mathcal{B(H)}
are invertible. Thus, for each
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, an element
\mu\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
defined by
\mathds{1}-\mu=\lambda
has the property that
\mathrm{Re}(\mu_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. The stated results follow.∎

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{M}_{d}
be a complex Hilbert space and let
\mathcal{N}
be a closed subspace of
\mathcal{M}
. Let
X=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d}),B=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{d})
and
Y=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d})
be
d
-tuples of operators as in Lemma
5.3
, where
X_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N})
,
Y_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp})
and
B_{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},\mathcal{N})
for all integers such that
1\leq j\leq d
, and the following conditions are satisfied

(1)

0\leq X_{j}\leq 1
,
0\leq Y_{j}\leq 1
;

(2)

\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}},~\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0,~\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
;

(3)

B_{i}Y_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}-X_{i}B_{j}
,
B_{i}^{*}X_{j}=\delta_{ij}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{i}B_{j}^{*}
;

(4)

B_{i}B^{*}_{j}=\delta_{ij}X_{j}-X_{i}X_{j},~B^{*}_{i}B_{j}=\delta_{ij}Y_{j}-Y_{i}Y_{j}
.

Then for all
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

\mu_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}=\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}

(5.21)

and

\lambda_{Y}+\lambda_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)^{-1}\lambda_{B}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}.

(5.22)

Proof.

We firstly prove equation (
5.21
). By assumption (2),
\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}}
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{\mu_{i}}{1-\lambda_{i}}B_{i}^{*}\bigg)\bigg(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}X_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{\mu_{i}}{1-\lambda_{i}}B_{i}^{*}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})X_{j}\bigg).

Thus

\displaystyle\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}

\displaystyle\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}B_{1}^{*}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})X_{j}+\ldots+\dfrac{\mu_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}B_{d}^{*}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})X_{j}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})B_{1}^{*}X_{j}+\ldots+\dfrac{\mu_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})B_{d}^{*}X_{j}.

(5.23)

By equation (
5.6
), equation (
5.23
) can be written

\displaystyle\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})\Big[\delta_{1j}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{1}B_{j}^{*}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\ldots+\dfrac{\mu_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})\Big[\delta_{dj}B_{j}^{*}-Y_{d}B_{j}^{*}\Big]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}\bigg((1-\lambda_{1})(1-Y_{1})B_{1}^{*}-(1-\lambda_{2})Y_{1}B_{2}^{*}-\ldots-(1-\lambda_{d})Y_{1}B_{d}^{*}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\ldots+\dfrac{\mu_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\bigg(-(1-\lambda_{1})Y_{d}B_{1}^{*}-(1-\lambda_{2})Y_{d}B_{2}^{*}-\ldots+(1-\lambda_{d})(1-Y_{d})B_{d}^{*}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}B_{1}^{*}-\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}\bigg(Y_{1}(1-\lambda_{1})B_{1}^{*}+\ldots+Y_{1}(1-\lambda_{d})B_{d}^{*}\bigg)+

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\ldots+\mu_{d}B_{d}^{*}-\dfrac{\mu_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\bigg((1-\lambda_{1})Y_{d}B_{1}^{*}+\ldots+(1-\lambda_{d})Y_{d}B_{d}^{*}\bigg).

With Definition
5.1
applied appropriately to each operator, we deduce that

\displaystyle\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)

\displaystyle=\mu_{B^{*}}-\Bigg[\dfrac{\mu_{1}Y_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}\bigg((1-\lambda_{1})B_{1}^{*}+\ldots+(1-\lambda_{d})B_{d}^{*}\bigg)+\ldots

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\dfrac{\mu_{d}Y_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\bigg((1-\lambda_{1})B_{1}^{*}+\ldots+(1-\lambda_{d})B_{d}^{*}\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mu_{B^{*}}-\bigg[\dfrac{\mu_{1}Y_{1}}{1-\lambda_{1}}+\ldots+\dfrac{\mu_{d}Y_{d}}{1-\lambda_{d}}\bigg](\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{B^{*}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{B^{*}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{B^{*}}.

(5.24)

By assumption (2),
\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0
so that

(\mathds{1}-\lambda)_{B^{*}}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})B_{j}^{*}=-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}B_{j}^{*}=-\lambda_{B^{*}}.

Therefore, equation (
5.24
) can be rearranged to

\mu_{B^{*}}=\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X}\big)-\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}}.

(5.25)

Take
\mu=\mathds{1}
in equation (
5.25
) to obtain

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}}.

By Theorem
5.5
,
(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}
and
\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\Big)_{Y}^{-1}
exist, therefore

\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}.

(5.26)

Multiply equation (
5.25
) on the right by
(1-\lambda_{X})^{-1}
and then use equation (
5.26
) to show the validity of equation (
5.21
), namely

\displaystyle\mu_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mu}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}.

To prove equation (
5.22
), let
\mu=\lambda
in equation (
5.21
) to obtain

\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}=\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}.

Then, multiplying on the right by
\lambda_{B}
, we have

\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}\lambda_{B}=\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}-\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}.

(5.27)

By some subtle manipulations, we can reduce equation (
5.27
) as follows. Since

\displaystyle\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}-(\mathds{1}-\lambda)}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B},

and, by assumption (2),
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{d}B_{j}=0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}+\Big(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}\Big)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}-(\mathds{1}-\lambda)}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}.

(5.28)

Now, for each term in the operator
\mu_{B^{*}}
, let
\mu_{j}=\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, so that

\mu_{B^{*}}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mu_{j}B_{j}^{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}B_{j}^{*}=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}.

From Proposition
5.4
, use the identity
\mu_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}=(\mu\lambda)_{Y}-\mu_{Y}\lambda_{Y}
with
\mu_{j}=\dfrac{1}{1-\lambda_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
to see that

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}=\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\lambda_{Y}.

Thus, equation (
5.28
) reduces to

\displaystyle\lambda_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-\lambda_{X})^{-1}\lambda_{B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg[\bigg(\dfrac{\lambda}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}\lambda_{Y}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}-(\mathds{1}-\lambda)}{1-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}-\lambda_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}-\lambda_{Y},

from which, after rearranging terms, we find equation (
5.22
).∎

6.
Analyticity of some operator-valued functions on
\mathbb{D}^{d}

In Definition
1.7
of a generalisation of models of functions from
\mathcal{SA}_{d}
we consider operator-valued functions

I(\cdot)
. The study of properties of operator-valued functions
I(\cdot)
shall be the objective of this section.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a Hilbert space. We say that

f:\mathbb{D}^{d}\to\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})

is a contractive analytic
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
-valued function on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
if
f
is analytic on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
and, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

\|f(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}\leq 1.

We were introduced to our first result for operator-valued functions on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
of the form
(f(\lambda))_{T}
with Theorem
5.5
. We now expand upon that theorem and include some essential results which will be used in later sections. For the following results, we define

\mathbb{C}_{+}^{d}=\big\{(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}:\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})>0~~\text{for}~~j=1,\ldots,d\big\}.

Recall Definition
5.1
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a complex Hilbert space and let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
. Then if
T=(T_{1},T_{2},\ldots,T_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of operators on
\mathcal{M}
, we recall the operator

\lambda_{T}=\lambda_{1}T_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}T_{d}

(6.1)

.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
T=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
be a d-tuple of operators on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}T_{j}=1_{\mathcal{H}}
and
0\leq T_{j}\leq 1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. Then

(1)

the map
\lambda\mapsto\lambda_{T}
from
\mathbb{C}^{d}
into
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
is analytic;

(2)

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}
is analytic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
;

(3)

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}
is analytic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{+}
;

(4)

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}
is analytic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
.

Proof.

We shall show that each of the operators above is analytic for their respective domains. To prove (1), let
f:\mathbb{C}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(H)}
be defined by
\lambda\mapsto\lambda_{T}
. We claim that, for every
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

f^{\prime}(\lambda):\mathbb{C}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(H)}:x\mapsto x_{T}

is the Fréchet derivative. Note, for any
\lambda
and
x
in
\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

(\lambda+x)_{T}=(\lambda_{1}+x_{1})T_{1}+\ldots+(\lambda_{d}+x_{d})T_{d},

and so
(\lambda+x)_{T}=\lambda_{T}+x_{T}.
Indeed,
f
is a bounded linear map from
\mathbb{C}^{d}
to
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
, and so
f
is its own Fréchet derivative.

To prove (2), for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the Fréchet derivative
D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}:\mathbb{C}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(H)}
is given by

D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}(x)=-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{x_{j}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}T_{j},~~\text{for}~x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}.

Note that, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
and for
x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
with
x\neq 0
such that
x_{j}\neq-\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda+x}\bigg)_{T}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}-D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}(x)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{x_{j}}{(\lambda_{j}+x_{j})(\lambda_{j})}T_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{x_{j}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}T_{j}\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{x_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}+x_{j}}-\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg)T_{j}\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{-x_{j}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}(\lambda_{j}+x_{j})}T_{j}\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{x_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg\rvert^{2}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}+x_{j}}\bigg\rvert\|T_{j}\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{x_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg\rvert^{2}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}+x_{j}}\bigg\rvert.

(6.2)

Since
\lambda,x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
are such that
x_{j}\neq-\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
and
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have
\lambda+x\neq 0
. Thus the summation of inequality (
6.2
) is bounded. For
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\displaystyle\lvert x_{j}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\lvert x_{j}\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\infty}.

Thus, for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\lvert x_{j}\rvert}\leq\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\infty}},

therefore we have

\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}+x_{j}}\bigg\rvert\leq\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\lvert x_{j}\rvert}\leq\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\infty}}.

It is easy to see that
\|x\|_{\infty}\leq\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\leq\sqrt{d}\|x\|_{\infty}
. Thus for
x_{j}\neq-\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\infty}}\leq\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}.

Therefore from inequality (
6.2
),

\displaystyle\lim_{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{1}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda+x}\bigg)_{T}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}-D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}(x)\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(H)}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{\|x\|^{2}_{\infty}}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg\rvert^{2}\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{\|x\|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg\rvert^{2}\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\rightarrow 0}\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg\rvert^{2}\dfrac{1}{\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert-\|x\|_{\mathbb{C}^{d}}}

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}
is analytic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
with

D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}(x)=-\bigg(\dfrac{x}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)_{T}

for
x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
x_{j}\neq-\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
.

Let us prove (3). By Corollary
5.6
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}
is invertible. Note that, by

[
8
, Proposition I.2.6]
, the map
g:\mathrm{Inv}(\mathcal{B(H)})\rightarrow\mathrm{Inv}(\mathcal{B(H)})
such that
g:x\rightarrow x^{-1}
is analytic. Moreover, we have already shown in (2) that
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}
is analytic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}_{+}^{d}
. Observe that

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}=g\circ\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)_{T}.

Since the composition of analytic maps is also analytic, then
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}
is analytic. By the chain rule for Fréchet derivatives

[
6
, Proposition 5.3.7]
,

D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}(x)=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}\bigg(\dfrac{x}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)_{T}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}

for all
x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{+}
.

Let us show (4). By Corollary
5.6
, for
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\mu_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the operator
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mu}\bigg)_{T}
is invertible and

\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mu}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}

is analytic by (3). Thus, for every element
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
such that
\mathrm{Re}(\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,
1-\lambda
has the property that
\mathrm{Re}(1-\lambda_{j})>0
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. Therefore the inverse of the operator
\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)_{T}
exists. The analyticity of the operator then follows similarly to (3) with

D\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}(x)=-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}\bigg(\dfrac{x}{(\mathds{1}-\lambda)^{2}}\bigg)_{T}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{T}

(6.3)

for
x\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{+}
.∎

7.
Properties of a special operator-valued function on
\mathbb{D}^{d}

We can now state and prove our main results. In the proofs that follow, recall the operator
\lambda_{P}
on the Hilbert space
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{L}_{d}
, that is, let
P=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{d})
be the
d
-tuple of operators on
\mathcal{L}
where
P_{j}:\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}
is the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{L}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. Then we defined

\lambda_{P}=\lambda_{1}P_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}P_{d}.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a complex Hilbert space and let
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
. Define

I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}~~\text{for}~\lambda\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}~\text{such that}~\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}~\text{for}~j=1,\ldots,d,

(7.1)

where
(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d})
is a
d
-tuple of positive contractions on
\mathcal{M}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}}
. Then the following properties are satisfied.

(1)

I
is analytic on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
;

(2)

\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}<1
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
;

(3)

at every point
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,
I(\lambda)
exists,

I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)=I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}~~\text{and}~~\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}=1;

(4)

I:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(M)}
is an inner operator-valued function.

Proof.

Let us prove statement (1). For all
\lambda\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
,
\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})<1
for
j=1,2,\ldots,d
. By Proposition
6.2
(iv), the operator
I(\lambda)
is well defined on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}
except when
\lambda_{j}=\tau_{j}
for some
j=1,2,\ldots,d
and analytic on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
. By Propositon
6.2
(iv) and equation (
6.3
), the Frëchet derivative of
I(\lambda)
is given by

\displaystyle DI(\lambda)(h)

\displaystyle=-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{\overline{\tau_{j}}h_{j}}{(1-\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})^{2}}Y_{j}\bigg)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}

\displaystyle=-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{{\tau}^{*}h}{(\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda)^{2}}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}.

(7.2)

Therefore by equation (
7.2
),
I(\lambda)
is analytic on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
and at each
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,d.
. Thus
I
is analytic on
\mathbb{D}
and analytic almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
.

We prove statement (2), that is,
\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}<1
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
; note that

\displaystyle 1_{\mathcal{M}}-I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\Bigg[1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg]^{*}\Bigg[1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}+\Bigg[\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg]^{*}-\Bigg[\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg]^{*}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg[1_{\mathcal{M}}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg[\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}}\Bigg]\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}.

(7.3)

One can check that

\displaystyle\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}+\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{1}{1-\tau_{i}\overline{\lambda_{i}}}Y_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\tau_{i}}\lambda_{i}}Y_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\tau_{i}\overline{\lambda_{i}}}+\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\tau_{i}}\lambda_{i}}-1\bigg)Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{1-\lvert\lambda_{i}\rvert^{2}}{\lvert 1-\overline{\tau_{i}}\lambda_{i}\rvert^{2}}Y_{i}=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}-\lvert\lambda\rvert^{2}}{\lvert\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda\rvert^{2}}\bigg)_{Y}>0

(7.4)

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. Then equation (
7.3
) and equation (
7.4
) imply

1_{\mathcal{M}}-I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)=\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\bigg(\dfrac{1-\lvert\lambda\rvert^{2}}{\lvert\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda\rvert^{2}}\bigg)_{Y}\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}.

Hence
1_{\mathcal{M}}-I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)
is of the form
X^{*}AX
, where
X,A\in\mathcal{B(N^{\perp})}
,
X
is invertible and
A
is a strictly positive operator. Thus, by
[
4
, Lemma 2.81]
,
1_{\mathcal{M}}-I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)>0
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
.

Let us prove statement (3). Rearrange equation (
7.1
) to read

I(\lambda)\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}},

and

\bigg(I(\lambda)\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}\bigg)^{*}=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}I^{*}(\lambda).

Then

\displaystyle\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}I^{*}(\lambda)

\displaystyle I(\lambda)\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}=\bigg[\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}}\bigg]\bigg[\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}+1_{\mathcal{M}}\hskip 142.26378pt

\displaystyle=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}+1_{\mathcal{M}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{1-\tau_{j}\overline{\lambda_{j}}}+\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j}}\bigg)Y_{j}

\displaystyle=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}+1_{\mathcal{M}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{2(1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j}))}{\lvert 1-\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}Y_{j}.

Since
\tau\neq\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
is such that
\tau_{j}\neq\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have
\lvert 1-\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}=2(1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda_{j}))
. By assumption,
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}}
. Hence, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}

and so

0=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}\Big[1_{\mathcal{M}}-I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)\Big]\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}.

(7.5)

By Theorem
5.5
, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
with
\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda_{j})<1
and
\mathrm{Re}(\tau_{j}\overline{\lambda_{j}})<1
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, the operators

\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}~~\text{and}~~\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y},

are invertible. We guarantee these conditions for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
. Therefore upon multiplying equation (
7.5
) by the appropriate invertible operators and rearranging it, we obtain

I^{*}(\lambda)I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}.

(7.6)

Likewise, if we intend to calculate

I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)=\bigg(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)^{-1}_{Y}\bigg)\bigg(1_{\mathcal{M}}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)^{-1}_{Y}\bigg),

(7.7)

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, then let us do so by firstly expanding equation (
7.7
) and then multiplying from the right and the left as below

I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}=\big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}-1_{\mathcal{M}}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)^{-1}_{Y}\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}+\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}^{-1},

and

\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}\big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}+1_{\mathcal{M}}.

Therefore, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\tau_{j}\neq\lambda_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\displaystyle\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}+\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}\big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\dfrac{2(1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda_{j}))}{\lvert 1-\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda{j}\rvert^{2}}Y_{j}+\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}\big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}.

Since
\lvert 1-\tau_{j}\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}=2(1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau}_{j}\lambda_{j}))
and
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}}
, we have

\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\bigg)_{Y}=\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)_{Y}\big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\tau\overline{\lambda}}\Big)_{Y}.

(7.8)

Thus, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,d
,

I(\lambda)I^{*}(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}.

Together, equations (
7.6
) and (
7.8
) prove that
I(\lambda)
is unitary for
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
.

Let us show that the radial limit
\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\tau}I(r\tau)
exists. Indeed,

\displaystyle\|I(r\tau)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\tau r}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}-1_{\mathcal{M}}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\tau r}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\dfrac{1}{1-r}Y_{i}\bigg)^{-1}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|(1-r)\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d}Y_{i}\bigg)^{-1}\Bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}.

By the assumption
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}}
, and so

\displaystyle\|I(r\tau)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}=1-r.

Therefore we have

\lim_{r\rightarrow 1^{-}}\|I(r\tau)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}=0.

Thus,
I:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(M)}
defined by

I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}

is an inner operator-valued function. ∎

8.
Boundary behaviour of
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
via desingularized models

In this section, for a general positive integer
d
and for a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
with a carapoint
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, we construct a desingularized model
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
of
\varphi
relative to
\tau
,
see Definition
1.10
.

Let us recall the definition of a generalized model of an analytic function
\varphi:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

Definition 8.1
.

[
5
, Definition 3.1]

Let
\varphi:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be analytic. The triple
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
is said to be a generalized model of
\varphi
if

(1)

\mathcal{M}

is a separable Hilbert space
;

(2)

u:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}

is an analytic mapping
;

(3)

I

is a contractive analytic

\mathcal{B(M)}
-valued function on

\mathbb{D}^{d}
such that
\|I(\lambda)\|<1
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
;

(4)

For all

\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

1-\overline{\varphi(\mu)}\varphi(\lambda)=\Big\langle\big(1_{\mathcal{M}}-I(\mu)^{*}I(\lambda)\big)u({\lambda}),u({\mu})\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{M}}.

(8.1)

Let
I:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(M)}
be an analytic mapping.
We say
I(\cdot)

extends analytically
to the point
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
if there exists an open neighbourhood
U
of
\lambda
and an analytic
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{M})
-valued function
F
on
U
such that
I
agrees with
F
on
U\cap\mathbb{D}^{d}
.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
be a carapoint for
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
. There exists a generalized model
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
of
\varphi
such that

(1)

\tau
is a
C
-point for
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
;

(2)

at every point
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,
I
extends analytically to
\lambda
and
\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}=1
;

(3)

\tau
is a carapoint for
I
and
\lim_{\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau}I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}
;

(4)

I:\mathbb{D}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{B(M)}
is an inner
\mathcal{B(M)}
-valued function.

Moreover, we can express
I
in the form

I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1},\;\text{for all}\;\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d},

(8.2)

and for some
d
-tuple of positive contractions
(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d})
on
\mathcal{M}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}=1_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Proof.

By
[
4
, Theorem 9.102]
, every function
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
has a model
(\mathcal{L},v)
. Choose any model
(\mathcal{L},v)
of
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
and any realization
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,D)
of
(\mathcal{L},v)
. By definition,
\mathcal{L}
is a separable Hilbert space with orthogonal decomposition
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{L}_{d}
. Let
P=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{d})
be the
d
-tuple of operators on
\mathcal{L}
where
P_{j}:\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}
is the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{L}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
.

Define
\mathcal{N}=\mathrm{Ker}(1-D\tau_{P})
, where
\tau_{P}=\tau_{1}P_{1}+\ldots+\tau_{d}P_{d}
. Since
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
is a carapoint for
\varphi
, we may apply Lemma
5.2
to deduce that
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1-D\tau_{P})\subset\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
and
\overline{\tau}_{P}\beta\in\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
. Note that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
.

Firstly, consider the possibility that
\mathcal{N}=\{0\}
. Then
1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau
is injective, hence there exists a unique vector
v(\tau)\in\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}=0\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
such that,

(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})v(\tau)=\gamma.

(8.3)

Let
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}
be any sequence in
\mathbb{D}^{d}
that converges nontangentially to
\tau
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. We claim that
v(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow v(\tau)
. Suppose that
v(\lambda^{(n)})\nrightarrow v(\tau)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. By Corollary
4.3
,
\{v(\lambda^{(n)})\}
is a bounded sequence in
\mathcal{L}
. Recall that the closed ball in a Hilbert space is compact in the weak topology. Thus there exist a subsequence of
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}
such that
\{v(\lambda^{(n)})\}
is weakly convergent in
\mathcal{L}
to an
x\in\mathcal{L}
such that
x\neq v(\tau)
with

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}v(\lambda^{(n)})=x\in\mathcal{L}.

Moreover, by Proposition
4.4
,
\|v(\lambda^{(n)})-x\|_{\mathcal{L}}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Now,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P}^{(n)})v(\lambda^{(n)})=(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})x=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\\
\end{bmatrix},

but
v(\tau)
is the unique vector in
\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
such that
(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})v(\tau)=\gamma
and here,
x\neq v(\tau)
, then this contradicts the uniqueness of the vector
v(\tau)
in equation (
8.3
). Therefore
v(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow v(\tau)
. Moreover,
v(\lambda)
extends continuously to
S\cup\{\tau\}
for any set
S
that tends to
\tau
nontangentially. Hence
\tau
is a
C
-point for
(\mathcal{L},v)
. Therefore the conclusion of the theorem for the case
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})=\{0\}
follows if we take
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{L}
,
u=v
and
I(\lambda)=\lambda_{P}
.

Assume now that
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})\neq\{0\}
. With respect to the orthogonal decomposition
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
, where
\mathcal{N}=\mathrm{Ker}(1-D\tau_{P})
,
D\tau_{P}
has the operator matrix

D\tau_{P}=\begin{bmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\\
A_{3}&A_{4}\end{bmatrix},

for contractions
A_{1}\in\mathcal{B(N)}
,
A_{2}\in\mathcal{B(N^{\perp},N)}
,
A_{3}\in\mathcal{B(N,N^{\perp})}
and
A_{4}\in\mathcal{B(N^{\perp})}
. Let
w\in\mathcal{N}
and define
P_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}
to be the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{N}
and
D\tau_{P}|_{\mathcal{N}}
to be the restriction of
D\tau_{P}
to
\mathcal{N}
, that is,
D\tau_{P}|_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}
. Note that any
w\in\mathrm{Ker}(1-D\tau_{P})
if and only if
D\tau_{P}w=w
. Therefore, for any
w\in\mathcal{N}
,

w=P_{\mathcal{N}}D\tau_{P}|_{\mathcal{N}}w=P_{\mathcal{N}}\begin{bmatrix}A_{1}w\\
A_{3}w\end{bmatrix}=A_{1}w.

Hence
A_{1}=1_{\mathcal{N}}
. Therefore, in the contractive matrix
D\tau_{P}
, since
1_{\mathcal{N}}
is unitary, we have
A_{2}=A_{3}=0
. Let
Q=A_{4}
. so that
Q
is contraction on
\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
. Then

D\tau_{P}=\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}&0\\
0&Q\end{bmatrix}.

We claim that
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q)=\{0\}
. Suppose
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q)\neq\{0\}
: there exists a
u_{0}\in\mathcal{N^{\perp}}
such that
(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q)u_{0}=0
. However, for any

v\in\mathcal{L}
,
(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})v=0
if and only if

v\in\mathcal{N}
. Choosing
v=u_{0}
we deduce a contradiction, therefore
\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q)=\{0\}
.
Express
\lambda_{P}\in\mathcal{B(L)}
as an operator matrix with respect to the decomposition
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
as in Lemma
5.3
by

\lambda_{P}=\lambda_{1}P_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{d}P_{d}=\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}X_{j}&\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}B_{j}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}B_{j}^{*}&\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}Y_{j}\\
\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{X}&\lambda_{B}\\
\lambda_{B^{*}}&\lambda_{Y}\end{bmatrix}.

(8.4)

Likewise,

\tau_{P}=\begin{bmatrix}\tau_{X}&\tau_{B}\\
\tau_{B^{*}}&\tau_{Y}\end{bmatrix}~\text{and}~\overline{\tau}_{P}=\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\tau}_{X}&\overline{\tau}_{B}\\
\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}&\overline{\tau}_{Y}\end{bmatrix},

so that, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
,

\displaystyle 1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P}\tau_{P}^{*}\lambda_{P}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{L}}-\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}&0\\
0&Q\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\tau}_{X}\lambda_{X}+\overline{\tau}_{B}\lambda_{B^{*}}&\overline{\tau}_{X}\lambda_{B}+\overline{\tau}_{B}\lambda_{Y}\\
\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}\lambda_{X}+\overline{\tau}_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}}&\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}+\overline{\tau}_{Y}\lambda_{Y}\end{bmatrix}.

By the identities from Proposition
5.4
,

\displaystyle 1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{L}}-\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}&0\\
0&Q\\
\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}&(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\\
(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}&(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}&-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\\
-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}&1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}\\
\end{bmatrix}.

For the vector
v
in the model
(\mathcal{L},v)
of the function
\varphi
with
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
, let
w(\lambda)=P_{\mathcal{N}}v(\lambda)
and
u(\lambda)=P_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}v(\lambda)
for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
, so that
v(\lambda)=w(\lambda)+u(\lambda)
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
and

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P})\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}&-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\\
-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}&1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}\\
\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})w(\lambda)-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)\\
-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y})u(\lambda)\\
\end{bmatrix}

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. Hence the following system of equations holds,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})w(\lambda)-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)

(8.5)

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y})u(\lambda)

(8.6)

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. From equation (
8.5
),

(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})w(\lambda)=(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)

(8.7)

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
and, furthermore, Theorem
5.5
is applicable, with
\lambda
replaced by
\overline{\tau}\lambda
, so that

w(\lambda)=(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)

(8.8)

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. Therefore, by equations (
8.6
) and (
8.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y})u(\lambda)

\displaystyle=-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)+(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y})u(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\Big[(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y})-Q(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\Big]u(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\Big[1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q\Big((\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\Big)\Big]u(\lambda)

(8.9)

for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. Again with
\lambda
replaced by
\overline{\tau}\lambda
, we can use Proposition
5.7
, equation (
5.22
), to show that

(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}\big(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}\big)^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)_{Y}^{-1}.

(8.10)

Therefore equation (
8.10
) implies that equation (
8.9
) takes the form

\gamma=\bigg[1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-Q\Big(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\Big(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\Big)^{-1}_{Y}\Big)\bigg]u(\lambda)=\big(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-QI(\lambda)\big)u(\lambda),

(8.11)

where, for all
\lambda\in\overline{\mathbb{D}^{d}}
such that
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
, we define
I(\lambda)
by

I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}\in\mathcal{B(N^{\perp})}.

(8.12)

By Proposition
7.1
,
I
is analytic on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
,
\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}<1
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
and
\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}=1
at every point
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that, for all
j=1,\ldots,d
,
\lambda_{j}\neq\tau_{j}
.

Let us show that equation (
8.1
) holds for
\varphi
. For the vector
v(\lambda)
in the model
(\mathcal{L},v)
of the function
\varphi
with
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{N}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
, let
w(\lambda)=P_{\mathcal{N}}v(\lambda)
and
u(\lambda)=P_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}v(\lambda)
. Using equation (
8.4
), we can perform the calculation

\displaystyle\overline{\tau}_{P}\lambda_{P}v(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\overline{\tau}_{P}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{X}&\lambda_{B}\\
\lambda_{B^{*}}&\lambda_{Y}\\
\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\tau}_{X}&\overline{\tau}_{B}\\
\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}&\overline{\tau}_{Y}\\
\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{X}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{B}u(\lambda)\\
\lambda_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{Y}u(\lambda)\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\tau}_{X}(\lambda_{X}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{B}u(\lambda))+\overline{\tau}_{B}(\lambda_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{Y}u(\lambda))\\
\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}(\lambda_{X}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{B}u(\lambda))+\overline{\tau}_{Y}(\lambda_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+\lambda_{Y}u(\lambda))\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}(\overline{\tau}_{X}\lambda_{X}+\overline{\tau}_{B}\lambda_{B^{*}})w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}_{X}\lambda_{B}+\overline{\tau}_{B}\lambda_{Y})u(\lambda)\\
(\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}\lambda_{X}+\overline{\tau}_{Y}\lambda_{B^{*}})w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}_{B^{*}}\lambda_{B}+\overline{\tau}_{Y}\lambda_{Y})u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix}.

(8.13)

Once more, use the identities from Proposition
5.4
with
\lambda

replaced by
\overline{\tau}\lambda
in equation (
8.13
) to obtain

\overline{\tau}_{P}\lambda_{P}v(\lambda)=\begin{bmatrix}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)\\
(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}u(\lambda)\\
\end{bmatrix}_{\mathcal{N}\oplus{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}}.

(8.14)

Observe the following. By equation (
8.7
),

(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)=w(\lambda)-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}w(\lambda).

Therefore

(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}u(\lambda)=(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}w(\lambda)+w(\lambda)-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X}w(\lambda)=w(\lambda)

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. By equation (
8.8
) and equation (
8.10
),

(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}w(\lambda)+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}u(\lambda)=\Big((\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{Y}+(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B^{*}}(1_{\mathcal{N}}-(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{X})^{-1}(\overline{\tau}\lambda)_{B}\Big)u(\lambda)=I(\lambda)u(\lambda)

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
. Hence, by equation (
8.14
),

\overline{\tau}_{P}\lambda_{P}v(\lambda)=\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
I(\lambda)u(\lambda)\\
\end{bmatrix}

(8.15)

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
.
With equation (
8.15
), we can derive the generalized model formula from the simpler model formula of Definition
1.1
:

\displaystyle 1-\overline{\varphi(\mu)}\varphi(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\langle(1_{\mathcal{L}}-\mu_{P}^{*}\lambda_{P})v(\lambda),v(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\langle v(\lambda),v(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}-\langle\lambda_{P}v(\lambda),\mu_{P}v(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\langle\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}w(\mu)\\
u(\mu)\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}-\langle\tau_{P}\overline{\tau}_{P}\lambda_{P}v(\lambda),\mu_{P}v(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\langle w(\lambda),w(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}+\langle u(\lambda),u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\langle\overline{\tau}_{P}\lambda_{P}v(\lambda),\overline{\tau}_{P}\mu_{P}v(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\langle w(\lambda),w(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}+\langle u(\lambda),u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\Bigg\langle\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda)\\
I(\lambda)u(\lambda)\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}w(\mu)\\
I(\mu)u(\mu)\end{bmatrix}\Bigg\rangle_{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=\langle w(\lambda),w(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}+\langle u(\lambda),u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\langle w(\lambda),w(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}}-\langle I(\lambda)u(\lambda),I(\mu)u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\langle u(\lambda),u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-\langle I(\lambda)u(\lambda),I(\mu)u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\langle(1_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}-I^{*}(\mu)I(\lambda))u(\lambda),u(\mu)\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}.

(8.16)

Hence
(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},u,I)
is an inner generalized model for
\varphi
. The statement of the theorem holds if
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
,
u(\lambda)=P_{\mathcal{N}^{\perp}}v(\lambda)
and
I(\lambda)
is defined by equation (
8.12
).

We now prove that
\tau
is a
C
-point for the model
(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},u,I)
; that is, there exists a vector
u(\tau)
such that, for every sequence
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
that converges to
\tau
nontangentially,
u(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow u(\tau)
in norm as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Once more, by Corollary
4.3
,
\tau
is a
B
-point for the model
(\mathcal{L},v)
and
\gamma\in\mathrm{Ran}(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})
. Let
u(\tau)
be the unique element of smallest norm in the closed convex non-empty set

\Big\{x\in\mathcal{L}:(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})x=\gamma\Big\}.

Then
u(\tau)\in\mathrm{Ker}(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})^{\perp}
and every element of
(1-D\tau_{P})^{-1}\gamma
has the form
e\oplus u(\tau)
for some
e\in\mathcal{N}
.

Let
X_{\tau}
be the nontangential cluster set of
v(\lambda)
at
\tau
in the model
(\mathcal{L},v)
. By Proposition
4.4
, since
\tau
is a
B
-point for

\varphi
, a sequence
v(\lambda^{(n)})
converges in norm as
n\rightarrow\infty
if and only if it converges weakly in
\mathcal{L}
. With this, if
x\in X_{\tau}
is the limit of the vector
v(\lambda^{(n)})
as
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
,

(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P}^{(n)})v(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\end{bmatrix},

then

(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\lambda_{P}^{(n)})v(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})x~~\text{as}~n\rightarrow\infty

and

(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})x=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\end{bmatrix}.

Thus

x\in X_{\tau}\subset(1_{\mathcal{L}}-D\tau_{P})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\end{bmatrix}\subset\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}e\\
u(\tau)\\
\end{bmatrix}:e\in\mathcal{N}\bigg\}.

We claim that
u(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow u(\tau)
in norm for every sequence
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
. Suppose not, since
v(\lambda^{(n)})
is bounded, then clearly
w(\lambda^{(n)})
and
u(\lambda^{(n)})
are bounded. By passing to a subsequence if necessary, we can assume that
u(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow y\neq u(\tau)
, and likewise, by passing to a further subsequence, we may suppose that
v(\lambda^{(n)})
converges to some vector
x\in X_{\tau}
. Then

v(\lambda^{(n)})=\begin{bmatrix}w(\lambda^{(n)})\\
u(\lambda^{(n)})\end{bmatrix}\rightarrow x\in\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}e\\
u(\tau)\\
\end{bmatrix}:e\in\mathcal{N}\bigg\},

and therefore
u(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow u(\tau)
, which is a contradiction to our assumption. Hence, for every sequence
\{\lambda^{(n)}\}{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
,
u(\lambda^{(n)})\rightarrow u(\tau)
in norm. Therefore
\tau
is a
C
-point for the model
(\mathcal{N}^{\perp},u,I)
.

To prove
\tau
is a carapoint for
I
, let
0<r<1
and let
\lambda=r\tau
. Then

\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})=(r\tau_{1},\ldots,r\tau_{d}),

and

\lambda\overline{\tau}=r\tau\overline{\tau}=(r\tau_{1}\overline{\tau}_{1},\ldots,r\tau_{d}\overline{\tau}_{d})=r\cdot\mathds{1}.

By definition of
I(\lambda)
, see equation (
8.12
),

\displaystyle I(r\tau)

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}r\tau}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}-\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-r\cdot\mathds{1}}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}-\Bigg[\dfrac{1}{1-r}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{d}Y_{j}\bigg)\Bigg]^{-1}

\displaystyle=1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}-\Bigg[\dfrac{1}{1-r}\cdot 1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}\Bigg]^{-1}=1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}-(1-r)_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}=r\cdot 1_{\mathcal{N^{\perp}}}.

Hence

\liminf_{\lambda\rightarrow\tau}\dfrac{1-\|I(\lambda)\|_{\mathcal{B(N^{\perp})}}}{1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}}\leq\liminf_{r\rightarrow 1}\dfrac{1-\|I(r\tau)\|_{\mathcal{B(N^{\perp})}}}{1-\|r\cdot\mathds{1}\|_{\infty}}=\liminf_{r\rightarrow 1}\dfrac{1-r}{1-r}=1.

Therefore
\tau
is a carapoint for
I
. We can also show
\lim_{\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau}I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}
. Suppose that
\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
, then there exists a
c>0
such that

\|\lambda-\tau\|_{\infty}\leq c(1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}).

By Theorem
5.5
,

\displaystyle\|I(\lambda)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\bigg(\dfrac{\mathds{1}}{\mathds{1}-\overline{\tau}\lambda}\bigg)^{-1}_{Y}\bigg\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j=1,\ldots,d}\dfrac{\lvert 1-\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}{1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})}

\displaystyle=\max_{j=1,\ldots,d}\dfrac{\lvert\tau_{j}-\lambda_{j}\rvert^{2}}{1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})}.

For
\tau_{j}\in\mathbb{T}
and
\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{D}
for
j=1,\ldots,d
,

\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})\leq\lvert\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j}\rvert=\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert.

Thus

\displaystyle 1-\mathrm{Re}(\overline{\tau_{j}}\lambda_{j})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert

\displaystyle\geq\min_{j=1,\ldots,d}(1-\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert)

\displaystyle=1-\max_{j=1,\ldots,d}\lvert\lambda_{j}\rvert

\displaystyle=1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}.

Hence we have the inequality

\|I(\lambda)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}\leq\dfrac{\|\lambda-\tau\|^{2}_{\infty}}{1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}}.

By assumption,
\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau
. By Definition
2.2
, there exists a
c>0
such that
\|\lambda-\tau\|_{\infty}\leq c(1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty})
. Thus

\|I(\lambda)-1_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{\mathcal{B(M)}}\leq\|\lambda-\tau\|\cdot\dfrac{\|\lambda-\tau\|_{\infty}}{1-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}}\leq c\|\lambda-\tau\|_{\infty}.

By taking limits in the above equation, we find that
\lim_{\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau}I(\lambda)=1_{\mathcal{M}}
.∎

The model
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
constructed in Theorem
8.2

is often referred to as the
desingularization
of the model
(\mathcal{L},v)
at
\tau
, relative to a particular realization of
(\mathcal{L},v)
, see the formal Definition
1.10
.

Corollary 8.3
.

Let
\tau\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
be a carapoint for
\varphi\in\mathcal{SA}_{d}
. Then
\varphi
has a non-tangential limit at
\tau

\lim_{\lambda{\overset{nt}{\rightarrow}}\tau}\varphi(\lambda)=\omega.

Moreover,
|\omega|=1
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.2
, there exists a desingularized model
(\mathcal{M},u,I)
of
\varphi
at
\tau
, and so,

(1)

for all
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{D}^{d}
, the following equation holds
